## Decisions and boundaries

- UI revision cap, selected 2026-10-07: each selected UI change gets at most three implementation-and-check rounds, then visual source is frozen for user feedback. Keep verification focused on the selected defect, retain failures/untested results honestly and obtain fresh user feedback before another bounded visual change. FlyYouFools=false can describe an on-foot player or a manually mounted driver as well as a passenger; do not infer passenger state from that preference.   - Font research reference supplied by mcvaxius: [OmenTools FontManager, 2.1.7.1](https://github.com/xa-io/xa-xiv-docs/blob/main/XA/DR%20Research/decompiled/2.1.7.1/OmenTools/OmenTools.OmenService/FontManager.cs). Reviewed the matching local snapshot at `Z:\xa-xiv-docs\XA\DR Research\decompiled\2.1.7.1\OmenTools\OmenTools.OmenService\FontManager.cs` on 2026-10-04. Useful for managed atlas handles, glyph-range composition and game/icon merges; this file contains no Devanagari shaping or cluster-layout implementation and its default ranges omit Devanagari. Treat it as font-management research, not proof of Hindi support. The remote fetch returned 404; local findings do not establish current remote contents.

  - Supplied `Z:\logs\font\` review completed on 2026-10-05. Root independently inspected the 1802324-byte TTF table directory, Unicode cmaps and name records: PangMenZhengDao-XiXian / 庞门正道细线体 contains zero U+0900–U+097F mappings, no GSUB/GPOS tables, and none of the seven Hindi sample strings' required characters. The screenshot shows the same decorative face. It is unsuitable for Hindi; no font was installed or copied. The source notes additionally request saved first-load language selection. That behavior is not in the currently recorded decisions: default English values and MOM's automatically resolved account language cannot consistently prove an explicit choice. Reconcile fresh-install/existing-config behavior before adding any confirmation field.
  - Established shaping engines are available. Windows DirectWrite supplies glyph indices, clusters, advances, offsets and caret hit testing; HarfBuzz supplies shaping but still needs the same ImGui integration. Current native ImGui Unicode measurement/AddText and InputText callbacks do not provide a complete shaped-glyph and cluster-editing bridge. A bitmap overlay alone would retain incorrect native measurement/caret geometry. The deferred follow-up therefore needs a separate include/lean/skip decision for renderer/layout/editing integration before implementation; choosing Windows-native APIs avoids an external shaping package but still creates ongoing bridge maintenance. Keep Hindi unadvertised while that decision is pending.
  - Historical prepared integration choice, 2026-10-06 (Include subsequently selected and implemented): Include means a shared Windows DirectWrite-to-ImGui bridge for shaping, measurement, clipping and editing, verified on one control before any consumer rollout. It avoids a third-party shaping package but requires maintaining font/texture lifetimes and native-control integration. Lean alternative retains the current renderer and fourteen-language rollout without Hindi; Skip defers Hindi work. Both leave Hindi unavailable. This is a new rendering-machinery decision under the supplied global rules; neither the earlier investigation approval nor glyph presence authorizes the bridge. Include is recorded and implemented; preserve the unanswered I462 interview without duplicate prompts.
